| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# b-isa-spec-pe1 — K13's extra borrowed bit

Council proposal; published text and fixtures are unchanged. References:
[ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md) §§4.2–4.3, 5.6,
12.1 “CLAIM-REPLAY witness”, 12.2 “Component resolution (K69/K70)”.

## Exact defect and North Star scope

The §12.1 subject is exactly the two-row oracle `r = lift.add x a;
lift.retval r`, with no inherited controls and an unused interface
region. The printed tuples nevertheless contain `borrowed:["1"]`,
the widths include `borrowed:[1]`, and the prose counts a 25-bit D and
coverage `1/33554432`. The lifted opcode set in §4.2 supplies no qborrow
instruction. This subject has no checked dirty-bundle lifetime.
§12.2 K70 expressly rejects a borrowed position beyond the checked
borrow requirement. The example cannot pass the rule it illustrates.

This is an inconsistent normative example, **not a missing borrow
capability** or a reason to relax K70. §4.3/§5.6 already supplies dirty
allocation and borrowed-system preservation where applicable. North
Star §§3.1, 4.2, 5.1 remains reachable, including dirty ancillae and
entanglement with an external reference. Correct the example rather
than adding a fictitious oracle input component.

## Alternatives and concrete witnesses

For x=1, a=2, target=2, fault=3, cells=[2,1], B=2, the two rows return
payload 3 and fault code 0. The resulting complete tuple is
`bundles:["1","1","3"], borrowed:[], regions:[["2","1"]]`.
The full basis domain has 2+2+4+16 = 24 bits. A singleton declared
domain, sampled four times with replacement, covers one of 2^24 full
tuples and remains evidence. Singleton ranges consume no random words.

Changing a to 1 changes target to 0. Reducing B to 1 changes the payload
to 0 and the final fault word to 3 XOR 1 = 2. An identity expected row
fails. Dropping the borrowed coordinate changes none of these results.
An invented one-bit coordinate must still reject under K70, even if
the table preserves it perfectly or calls it the same name as a real
interface bundle.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Remove the extra borrowed coordinate and adjust D/fraction | Repairs the example without changing any machine/domain rule. Recommended. |
| Replace the subject by a checked borrowing action wrapper | Possible new example, but needs its actual body, frame, lifetimes and new domains; the printed two-row oracle cannot stand in for it. |
| Invent oracle borrowed inputs or infer them from unused labels | Changes the oracle ABI and breaks K70 positional resolution; unjustified. |
| Count a lift.perm_call callee's internal scratch as this oracle's borrowed interface | Needs a separate lifetime/tuple contract; no such call exists in this witness. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Within the single §12.1 CLAIM-REPLAY witness JSON block, replace **all
six** tuple occurrences of `"borrowed":["1"]` by `"borrowed":[]`,
and make these additional replacements:

```diff
-  "widths":{"bundles":[2,2,4],"borrowed":[1],"regions":[[8,8]]},
+  "widths":{"bundles":[2,2,4],"borrowed":[],"regions":[[8,8]]},
-  "sample_count":4,"distinct_samples":1,"covered_fraction":"1/33554432","verdict":"evidence"
+  "sample_count":4,"distinct_samples":1,"covered_fraction":"1/16777216","verdict":"evidence"
```

Replace the indicated prose immediately below that block:

```diff
-with a preserved one-bit dirty bundle and an unused two-cell,
-eight-bit `in` interface region; no inherited controls.
+with an unused two-cell, eight-bit `in` interface region; no borrowed
+lifetimes and no inherited controls.
-full 25-bit D (2+2+4+1+16 bits).
+full 24-bit D (2+2+4+16 bits).
-words and cover one of 33554432 full tuples.
+words and cover one of 16777216 full tuples.
```

Append to that witness paragraph:

```text
The empty borrowed array is required here: the two-row oracle has no
checked borrow lifetime. Adding a borrowed coordinate to this witness
rejects under K70; unchanged borrowed contents alone do not establish
that a lifetime exists.
```

The replacements operate on the exact phrases across line wrapping;
do not change the separate general tuple encoding, sampler, layout,
arguments, target/fault widths or K70 rule.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

The witness deliberately supplies no bound object/closure hashes, so
there are no printed subject digests to recompute. No source instruction,
interface or ref catalog changes. Any concrete certificate/table fixture
based on the invalid example must change its tuple/domain bytes and
coverage report; a certificate digest consequently changes. Consumers
following K70 already reject the old example. Do not change their
component resolver or call the repair a proof over dirty inputs.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

The read-only replay suite's `tests.test_replay.K13Witness` has four
focused tests using the repaired empty-borrowed relation, including the
changed argument, short budget, wrong row and missing-cell cases.
Ran it together with `QinplaceCoverage` using the bounded command in
[INDEX](INDEX.md): all 12 tests passed, exit 0. The replay fixture uses
ptr_width=32 rather than the printed legal 2-bit layout, so this run
checks the relation/domain behavior, not literal byte reproduction of
that layout. It is also an exhaustive singleton-slice test, not the
printed four-draw sampled packet. The finite INDEX checks independently
check the bit count, all six tuple repairs and sampling calculation.

For acceptance publish a complete bound certificate for the repaired
2-bit-layout oracle, with the exact four-draw sampler and all region
cells. Expect evidence `1/16777216`; reject the extra borrowed position,
unchanged-argument/budget rebinding, identity row and omitted cell.
Retain a separate genuine qborrow action with an entangled external
reference to test dirty preservation; it must not be folded into this
oracle's D. A small example fix does not discharge suite C's full-D T3.

## K entry and publication route

Recommend a **0.9 K13 witness erratum**, not a new oracle ABI or 0.10
deferral. Proposed decision row: `K13 erratum | §12.1 | remove nonexistent
borrowed coordinate; D=2^24, coverage=1/16777216 | pe1 | example repair`.
Council assigns any new ledger number; this proposal reserves none.
The semantic rule already belongs to accepted K70. Keep pe1 open until
the editor and conformance owners publish the repair.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and
North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4. This tuple belongs to one embedded device
subject and its selected root. A native host or another marked module
does not contribute a borrowed coordinate, interface cell or certificate
record to this subject. No container, launch, budget, handle or tape
change is needed; the shared note's explicit mixed-launch association
work remains. The native binary need not become an oracle to repair K13.

name: beads

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# Contract derivations 3 — council decision index

Five proposals, not published spec changes or consumer acceptance. Read
COMMON, repository CLAUDE/AGENTS, the Beads skill/prime, two first-pass
notes and two pass2 notes before derivation. All five full issue records
were read with bd. Direct sibling reads, including --readonly, failed
at the read-only embedded Dolt LOCK. Copies of the actual embedded
databases under `.tmp/sol-derive3/` then permitted `bd --readonly show`
for 4yg.1 and brt-vbi. No passive JSONL was imported or substituted for
the database. Source issues remain open for council/implementation work.

| Issue | Determination | Recommended publication |
|---|---|---|
| [bennett-arith-4yg.1](bennett-arith-4yg.1.md) | Valid FMA and binary128-conversion midpoints have no even candidate under the printed PPC rule; flags separately violate an existing rule | Dialect §13.1 parity repair in next 0.2 patch and 0.3 draft; no ISA K |
| [brt-vbi](brt-vbi.md) | Published all-width helper requirements lack a public delivery/collection protocol; actual i257 bodies also missing | Dialect §§12.8/19.1 offline interface and fixed point; no new ISA opcode/K |
| [b-isa-spec-pe1](b-isa-spec-pe1.md) | K13 example invents a borrowed lifetime that K70 rejects | 0.9 K13 example erratum: empty borrowed arrays, 24-bit D |
| [b-isa-spec-2qc](b-isa-spec-2qc.md) | Executor preflight and proof owner exist; certificate checker coverage duty is ambiguous | Explicit owner/site/context rule, coordinated with 0jq; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |
| [b-isa-spec-sqr](b-isa-spec-sqr.md) | Bundle entries in an mcx list have no explicit operand rule; current implementations disagree | Ordered bundle expansion, full wire arity/distinctness; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |

None is an excuse to reduce a format/width domain, omit an inverse
obligation, or declare a North Star capability inert. K13 is an example
defect rather than a new machine capability gap. The remaining findings
concern deterministic contract interpretation or delivery. All existing
SQLite, Julia, libc, reversible execution and demo-ladder obligations
remain. Existing beads and cross-repo requests already track the decisions;
no duplicate task or sibling tracker mutation is needed for this pass.

## Publication and combined decisions

[spec/README.md](../../../spec/README.md) requires a full version change
for semantic changes and council review. “0.9 K repair” in these notes
means a proposed use of the existing council repair process, **not
authority to silently amend a published contract**. Without explicit
in-version authorization, the 2qc/sqr semantic additions go to 0.10.
New version prefixes change affected object/interface/closure envelopes,
example identities and consumer pins. A same-version repair with unchanged
source bytes preserves their source identities but still invalidates
incompatible execution/schedule/proof assumptions. `ref@0.9` changes only
if its own catalog bytes/version change; none of these proposals requires
such a rewrite. Assign numeric K entries only at acceptance.

PPC parity and runtime delivery belong to the dialect because they define
LLVM numerical/helper lowering and build inputs. ISA 0.9/0.10 need no
new K for either. Publish the exact proposed dialect text in the next
available maintained patch and mirror it in 0.3; the proposal is not a
private runtime/driver agreement. The new helper protocol is itself
versioned with schema 1, independent of ISA subformat numbers.

For 2qc use the existing concrete invocation-context vocabulary, owner
closure selection and owner-local claim indices. Coordinate with
[pass2 0jq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md); do not create a second
inverse proof owner or replace tuple applicability by root budget equality.
For sqr retain the written source identity and resolve complete wire
operands before K78 primitive matching and scheduling. A parenthesized
indexed list is not currently a legal substitute under §16.2.

## Mixed fat object

Every note cites and applies
[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md), binding under
bennett-4ht and advisor 08:45Z (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). A native host
binary remains native; only marked device modules and their required
linked closures become ISA objects. Helper generation, comparison,
certificate inventory and invocation coverage bind those device roots,
with a separate root-relative context [] per root. They do not inventory
native main, host-only argv/envp setup or host-only libc. The shared note
names the exact remaining container/root/certificate association, launch,
budget/handle, tape/run and host-memory mapping changes. This pass neither
implements those mechanisms nor locks them out.

| `timeout 90 python3 -B -m unittest -v tests.test_replay.K13Witness tests.test_replay.QinplaceCoverage` from sibling checker-replay | Exit 0; 12 tests passed (4 K13, 8 coverage) |
| Matching-major LLVM18/23 `opt -passes=verify -disable-output` on runtime-width-request.ll, plus `llvm-nm --defined-only` on each live runtime bitcode | Exit 0; valid source, both required i257 definitions absent on both majors |
| Current replay CLI on the mcx-bundle-control certificate/object, `--max-elements 16` | Exit 0; relation claim proof, 16 elements, fraction 1; unrelated metadata remains outside checked scope |
| Job-built reference `check` on original mcx bundle witness | Exit 1, `§3.3: c must name one wire`; an expected existing implementation boundary |
| Scratch ccx equivalent, reference `check`, `hash`, then 16 forward and 16 adjoint basis calls | All pass; the two object/closure identities are listed in sqr |
| Extracted Python verification block below, `timeout 120 python3 -I -B -u -` under the 1 GiB bound | Exit 0; five-note/index structure, exact witnesses, 12 replay tests, both LLVM audits and all 32 reference executions passed |

The runtime modules actually audited had SHA-256
`cc8f318f2caf9d989ea64932cd177c3bf32e6c65c156b9949d3b49e4b94c999f`
(LLVM18) and
`964ea16513db1d54adbaa5a4c8124b1ac731fee8f487f34dc6e9e12bb9df5642`
(LLVM23). These are observed evidence pins, not a request to re-pin
consumers to missing helper bodies. The proposed canonical request
example in the finite block hashes to
`74e7a75be203bc350a18a84980258fa09a3e9936837e8aa67fb01532064cdd81`;
it is a wire-format example, not an implemented generator result.

The PPC script loads the producer's existing native test libraries. This
pass does not freshly compile them from the publication or infer full
arith conformance from them; its exact rational defect proof is independent
of their answers. Stored older checker verdicts are identified as stored
evidence, distinct from the newly built reference result. The focused
replay tests do not implement the proposed nested-context checker rule.

## Reproducible finite checks

Run the following shell block after the environment prefix. It extracts
the Python below from this index and verifies the actual notes plus
concrete witnesses; it creates only a scratch ccx object. The outer memory
bound is inherited by all child commands.

```sh
date -u
ulimit -v 1048576
ulimit -c 0
timeout 120 python3 -I -B -u - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-derive3/INDEX.md')
code = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(code, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

```python
from pathlib import Path
from fractions import Fraction as F
from itertools import product
import hashlib, json, os, re, subprocess, sys

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
WS = ROOT.parent
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-derive3'
JOB = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-derive3'
REF = JOB / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
REPLAY = WS / 'bennett-conformance/checker-replay'
WITNESS = REPLAY / 'witnesses/mcx-bundle-control'
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(JOB / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(JOB / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))
ids = ['bennett-arith-4yg.1', 'brt-vbi', 'b-isa-spec-pe1',
       'b-isa-spec-2qc', 'b-isa-spec-sqr']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {i+'.md' for i in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    s = (OUT / (ident+'.md')).read_text()
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
    for heading in ('defect', 'north star', 'alternatives', 'recommended exact',
                    'hashes', 'conformance', 'k entry', 'mixed fat object'):
        assert heading in s.lower(), (ident, heading)
    assert 'fat-object-scope.md' in s
    assert index.count('| ['+ident+']') == 1
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
print('PASS: five proposals and index; sections, links, fences, fat-object citations')

def pow2(n):
    return F(2**n) if n >= 0 else F(1, 2**(-n))
def decode(bits, fraction_bits, exponent_bits, bias):
    sign = -1 if bits >> (fraction_bits+exponent_bits) else 1
    exp = (bits >> fraction_bits) & ((1 << exponent_bits)-1)
    frac = bits & ((1 << fraction_bits)-1)
    assert exp != (1 << exponent_bits)-1
    sig = frac if exp == 0 else (1 << fraction_bits) | frac
    return sign * sig * pow2((1-bias if exp == 0 else exp-bias)-fraction_bits)
def f64(bits):
    return decode(bits, 52, 11, 1023)
u = pow2(-1074)
h = f64(0x3ff0000000000001)
assert h == 1+pow2(-52)
exact = (h+u)*F(1,2) + f64(0x3fe0000000000001)
assert exact == h+u/2
H = f64(0x0030000000000001)
V = decode(0x3c030000000000001200000000000000, 112, 15, 16383)
assert V == H+u/2 and H > pow2(-1022)
for hi, v in ((h, exact), (H, V)):
    assert (hi/u).denominator == 1
    assert v-hi == hi+u-v == u/2
    assert hi+u-hi == u
# The two high raw significands and the nonzero low significand are odd.
assert (0x3ff0000000000001 & 1) == (0x0030000000000001 & 1) == 1
assert (0 & 1, 1 & 1) == (0, 1)  # proposed common low comparison selects zero
# Both-low-zero subnormal neighbours still use high parity.
assert f64(2)-f64(1) == u and (1 & 1, 2 & 1) == (1, 0)
print('PASS: independent exact PPC FMA/conversion midpoints and parity distinction')

spec = (ROOT / 'spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md').read_text()
block = spec.split('**CLAIM-REPLAY witness**', 1)[1].split('```json\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
assert block.count('"borrowed":["1"]') == 6
fixed = block.replace('"borrowed":["1"]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('"borrowed":[1]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('1/33554432', '1/16777216')
k13 = json.loads(fixed)
tuples = [k13['row']['before'], k13['row']['after'],
          k13['wrong_row']['before'], k13['wrong_row']['after'],
          k13['changed_arg_after'], k13['short_budget_after']]
assert all(t['borrowed'] == [] and t['regions'] == [['2','1']] for t in tuples)
assert k13['widths']['borrowed'] == []
bits = sum(k13['widths']['bundles']) + sum(sum(r) for r in k13['widths']['regions'])
assert bits == 24 and str(F(1, 1 << bits)) == k13['covered_fraction']
assert 2 ^ ((1+2) % 4) == 1 and 2 ^ ((1+1) % 4) == 0
assert (2 ^ 0, 3 ^ 1) == (2, 2)
assert k13['sample_count'] == 4 and k13['distinct_samples'] == 1
print('PASS: all six K13 tuple repairs; 24-bit domain and exact singleton coverage')

# A finite coverage counterexample, not an implementation of the checker.
minimum = (3,0,0,0)
def covers(tk, arg, domain=range(4), token_arg=None):
    return (all(a <= b for a,b in zip(minimum,tk)) and arg in domain
            and (token_arg is None or token_arg == (3,0,0,0)))
assert covers((3,0,0,0),1) and covers((9,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((2,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((3,0,0,0),3,range(3))
assert not covers((9,0,0,0),1,token_arg=(4,0,0,0))
contexts = [(3,0,0,0), (2,0,0,0)]
assert [covers(t,1) for t in contexts] == [True,False]
assert {('G','H'), ('H','G')} == {('H','G'), ('G','H')}
print('PASS: qinplace minimum, exact-token, args-domain and two-context counterexamples')

# Proposed wire-request example; arithmetic is independent of any runtime body.
mask = (1 << 257)-1
a, b = (1 << 256)+3, 2
assert ((a*b) & mask) == 6
assert a.bit_count() == 3 and (((a*b) & mask).bit_count()) == 2
assert [min(64,257-64*k) for k in range(5)] == [64,64,64,64,1]
entries = [{'op':'mul','width':257}, {'op':'ctpop','width':257}, {'op':'mul','width':257}]
canonical_entries = [{'op':op,'width':w} for op,w in sorted({(e['op'],e['width']) for e in entries})]
assert canonical_entries == [{'op':'ctpop','width':257},{'op':'mul','width':257}]
request = {'schema':'bennett-runtimes-instantiations-1', 'llvm_major':18,
           'target_triple':'x86_64-unknown-linux-musl', 'data_layout':'e-p:64:64',
           'entries':canonical_entries}
canonical = json.dumps(request,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')).encode()
print('Proposed request example SHA-256:',hashlib.sha256(canonical).hexdigest())
print('PASS: i257 top-limb arithmetic and canonical request example')

def run(argv, cwd=ROOT, timeout=20, expected=0):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=cwd, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=timeout)
    assert p.returncode == expected, (argv,p.returncode,p.stdout,p.stderr)
    return p
native = run([sys.executable,'-I','-B','-u',WS/'bennett-arith/tests/ppc_zero_low_witness.py'])
assert native.stdout.count('flags=0;') == 2
assert native.stdout.count('flags=24;') == 2
print(native.stdout, end='')
tests = run([sys.executable,'-B','-m','unittest','-v',
             'tests.test_replay.K13Witness','tests.test_replay.QinplaceCoverage'],cwd=REPLAY)
assert 'Ran 12 tests' in tests.stderr and tests.stderr.rstrip().endswith('OK')
print('PASS: independent replay focused tests, 12/12')
runtime = WS/'bennett-runtimes'
ll = runtime/'.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/tmp/runtime-width-request.ll'
for major in (18,23):
    tools = Path(f'/usr/lib/llvm-{major}/bin')
    run([tools/'opt','-passes=verify','-disable-output',ll])
    bc = runtime/f'dist/llvm{major}/bennett-runtimes.bc'
    nm = run([tools/'llvm-nm','--defined-only',bc]).stdout
    defs = {line.split()[-1] for line in nm.splitlines() if line.split()}
    assert not {'__bennett_rt_i257_mul','__bennett_rt_i257_ctpop'} & defs
    print('PASS: LLVM',major,'i257 source verifies; both definitions absent;',
          'runtime SHA-256',hashlib.sha256(bc.read_bytes()).hexdigest())

replay = json.loads(run([sys.executable,'-B','-m','checker_replay',
                        WITNESS/'certificate.json','--object',WITNESS/'object.bisa',
                        '--max-elements','16'],cwd=REPLAY).stdout)
claim = next(iter(replay['regions'].values()))['claims'][0]
assert (claim['outcome'],claim['elements'],claim['covered_fraction']) == ('proof',16,'1')
original = run([REF,'check',WITNESS/'object.bisa'],expected=1)
assert 'c must name one wire' in original.stderr
scratch = JOB/'tmp/mcx-ccx.bisa'
source = (WITNESS/'object.bisa').read_text()
old = 'e38d5da64f9a0ae89a66243bcbe5a321cb807a5bbabf83952b63d3465dfab346'
new = 'f53238499e07824b6c62385c33426d4bc56bfaf900df61264ec65e0589061f39'
assert source.count('mcx (c) p') == 1 and source.count(old) == 1
scratch.write_text(source.replace('mcx (c) p','ccx c[0] c[1] p').replace(old,new))
for command in ('check','hash'):
    result = json.loads(run([REF,command,scratch]).stdout)
    assert result['object_hash'] == 'c785ff3f310f968e2a26f68834e99ab892cf0d1bffe816cf26b17d45c33a65af'
    assert result['symbols']['a']['interface_hash'] == new
    assert result['symbols']['a']['closure_hash'] == new
for p,q,c in product(range(2),range(2),range(4)):
    result = json.loads(run([REF,'run-action',scratch,'a','--mut',f'{p},{q}','--in',str(c)]).stdout)
    assert result['mut'] == [q ^ int(c==3),p] and result['in'] == [c]
    back = json.loads(run([REF,'run-action',scratch,'a','--mut',
                          ','.join(map(str,result['mut'])),'--in',str(c),'--adjoint']).stdout)
    assert back['mut'] == [p,q] and back['in'] == [c]
print('PASS: mcx replay proof; original reference rejection; ccx check/hash and 16+16 executions')
print('PASS: derive3 verification complete; proposals remain unaccepted/unimplemented')
```

# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.
# Contract derivations — review index and reproducible verification

These are proposals for council grading, not published spec changes.
Contract beads remain open for acceptance/implementation; b5b's owner
handles its implementation status. No git command was run by this job.

A recommendation for a 0.9 K repair means the existing council-reviewed
repair track, not permission to publish an incompatible change silently.
`spec/README.md` normally requires a full-version bump for semantic
changes. Council must settle that publication choice. Every statement
below that existing source hashes stay fixed assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged envelope bytes; a full-version bump
instead changes all symbol/interface/closure envelopes and their consumer
pins. Catalog bytes/hash change only when that separately versioned
catalog is actually changed. No note here performs either publication.

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
python3 -I -B - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md')
source = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(source, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

# Owned deliverable structure and local links.
base = Path('docs/notes')
ids = ['z4c', 'l2p', 'fd0', 'lpe', 'adg', 'xv4', 'b5b', '5u3',
       'checker-native-model', 'checker-diagnostics', 'checker-fault-rate',
       'checker-resource-contexts']
for ident in ids:
    path = base / ('2026-11-02-contract-' + ident + '.md')
    body = path.read_text()
    for word in ['Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                 'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes',
                 'Conformance', 'Release']:
        assert word.lower() in body.lower(), (ident, word)
    assert not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', body)
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in body.splitlines()) % 2 == 0
    for target in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', body):
        if not target.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#')):
            assert (path.parent / target.split('#')[0]).exists(), (ident, target)
print('PASS: 12 proposals; exact AND 8 columns/32 inverse columns; '
      '128 corrected branch columns; 3 negative channels; '
      'model/domain/orbit/resource arithmetic; fences and local links')
print('Fixed-image SHA-256:', image_hash)
```

## Verification limits

The exact checks establish the finite witnesses and internal document
consistency. They do not prove the proposed general contract sound in
all cases, accept a new K entry, close an implementation bead, or show
that SQLite's full reference analysis converges in practical time.
Each item states its own remaining acceptance vectors and consumer work.

exec
/bin/bash -lc "cat spec/README.md
rg -n '"'^#{1,5} |CLAIM-REPLAY witness|K13|K69|K70|borrowed position|Component resolution|borrow|T3'"' spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md" in /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec
 succeeded in 0ms:
# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

### 4.2 Lifted rows (oracle bodies and lprocs)

Columns: **Class**, **Reads**, **Writes / slots** (A: displaced values in
slot order; D: the permutation), **Flag / fault** (C = code), **Succ**.
Every row is one budget step; `k` is its own index.

| Row | Form | Class | Reads | Writes / slots | Flag / fault | Succ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lift.const` | `w = lift.const.iN c` | A | – | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.binop` | `w = lift.{add,…,ashr} a b` | A | a, b | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.div` | `w = lift.{udiv,urem,sdiv,srem} a b` | A | a, b | w (under ¬flag) | flag = (b=0) ∨ (signed ∧ a=MIN ∧ b=−1); C = `FAULT_UB_DIV0` / `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.icmp` | `w = lift.icmp.{pred} a b` | A | a, b | w:reg[1] | – | k+1 |
| `lift.select` | `w = lift.select p a b` | A | p, a, b | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.cast` | `w = lift.{zext,sext,trunc}.iM a` | A | a | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.mov` | `w = lift.mov a` | A | a | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.parmov` | `(w_1..w_m) = lift.parmov (a_1..a_m)` | A | a_i | w_1..w_m (m slots) | – | k+1 |
| `lift.label` | `w = lift.label ^b` | A | – | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.addr` | `w = lift.addr @g` | A | – | w = layout base of global or mapped region g | – | k+1 |
| `lift.boundscheck` | `lift.boundscheck p n` | D | p, alloctab | – | flag = ¬inrange(p, n); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.load` | `w = lift.load.iN a` | A | the N/8 cells at a.. (§7.2) | w (under ¬flag) | flag = ¬inrange(a, N/8); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.store` | `lift.store.iN a v` | A | v | the N/8 cells at a.. (under ¬flag; one slot of N bits) | flag = ¬inrange(a, N/8); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.alloc` | `w = lift.alloc n` | A | n, heap_used, atop | under ¬flag, slots in this order: w ← heap_base + heap_used; alloctab[atop] ← (heap_base + heap_used, n, 1, 0); heap_used ← heap_used + max(n,1); atop ← atop + 1 (4 slots, all computed from the old state, then exchanged in this order, `alloctab[atop]` at the old `atop`). **Inverse exchanges**: atop, heap_used, alloctab[atop] (at the restored `atop`), w; then clear the four slots | flag = (heap_used + max(n,1) > heap_limit) ∨ (atop ≥ 2^A_tab); C = `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.free` | `lift.free p` | D | p, alloctab | match_i = (i < atop) ∧ (base_i = p) ∧ ¬pinned_i into scratch (at most one i matches: unpinned entries are heap entries, whose bases strictly increase); under ¬flag: live_i ^= match_i for all i; scratch uncomputed from the unchanged atop, bases and pinned bits | flag = ¬∃ i: match_i ∧ live_i; C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.faultif` | `lift.faultif p code` | D | p | – | flag = p; C = code, written as its effective cause C′ (§5.4) | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.call` | `w = lift.call @f (a_1..a_m) (tokens..) (classical..) (mem..)` (short form: §3.7) | A+D | §3.7 | §3.7 (slots: m capture slots `disp_1..disp_m`, then assignment slots for fp, sp, depth) | flag per §3.7; C = `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH` | ¬flag: entry of @f; flag: HALT |
| `lift.ret` | `lift.ret v` | A+D | §3.7 | §3.7 (slots: the delivery slot `disp_1`, then assignment slots for fp, depth) | flag = (depth = 0); C = `FAULT_UB_TRAP` | ¬flag: `succ`; flag: HALT |
| `lift.retval` | `lift.retval v` | D | v | result_val ^= v | – | HALT |
| `lift.oracle_call` | `lift.oracle_call @o tk (ins..) tgt fr (mem..) (args..)` | D | ins, tgt, fr, mem | tgt ^= payload; fr ^= code (callee contract) | – | k+1 |
| `lift.oracle_uncall` | same form | D | as above | the matched inverse (§5.5): the identical permutation | – | k+1 |
| `lift.perm_call` | `lift.perm_call @a (mut..) (ins..) (mem..) (args..)` | D | ins, mut, mem | mut ← P(mut, ins, mem); inverse = `adj` of @a | – | k+1 |
| `qjump` | `qjump ^b` | D | – | – | – | ^b |
| `qbranch` | `qbranch p ^b1 ^b2` | D | p | – | – | p: ^b1; ¬p: ^b2 |
| `qjumpr` | `qjumpr a` | D | a | succ ← a | flag = (a < first_f) ∨ (a ≥ first_f + n_f), where `[first_f, first_f + n_f)` are the row indices of the containing function instance (contiguous, §3.6); C = `FAULT_UB_TRAP` | ¬flag: `succ`; flag: HALT |
| `fault` | `fault C` | D | – | code ^= C′; faulted ^= 1 (C′ the effective cause of C, §5.4) | – | HALT |

**`inrange(p, n)`** = ∃ i < atop: live_i ∧ base_i ≤ p ∧ p + n ≤ base_i +
size_i, every comparison computed at width max(ptr_width, width(n)) + 1
(no wrap; §3.3); `lift.load`/`lift.store` carry this check
**inherently** on their own address and length, so the reference
function is total for every well-formed body; `lift.boundscheck` is an
explicit assertion row the dialect emits before each access (after it
the access's own flag is never set, so dialect-produced bodies' step
counts are unchanged). Rules: `lift.retval` is ill-formed in an lproc;
`lift.ret` is ill-formed outside lprocs; `tk` is a token-typed classical
argument of the calling oracle or a token parameter of the calling
lproc. `lift.oracle_call`/`uncall`/`perm_call` carry the
complete callee interface; `mem` operands name the calling oracle's
mapped interface regions or the calling lproc's region parameters.
`lift.addr @g` names a global anywhere; it names an
interface region **only in the body of the oracle that declares it**
(lprocs are shared between oracles and receive region bases as
parameters, as in §16.3 example 2). Label values obtained by `lift.
label` denote first rows of blocks of the containing function instance;
`qjumpr` to an index outside that instance's row range faults, so
control never leaves a function except through `lift.call`/`lift.ret`; an
in-range index that is not a block's first row enters that row. The
`args` operands of `lift.oracle_call`, `lift.oracle_uncall` and
`lift.perm_call` are classical arguments of the calling oracle or token
and classical parameters of the calling lproc, in callee signature
order and with exactly matching types, never locations. They are fixed
at entry (§3.1, §3.7). Every lifted call available in an oracle body is
also available in an lproc by passing the required token, classical
arguments and mapped regions explicitly through `lift.call`. In lifted rows
a width suffix `.iN`/`.iM` names a location width and has no upper bound
(§3.3), except that `lift.load.iN`/`lift.store.iN` keep N ∈ {8, 16, …,
128}.

**Reference body and binding.** Every oracle carries its **reference
body**: the stream the dialect's versioned mapping produced from the
materialised source; hashed into `interface_hash` (§9.2). F_B and the
budget unit are defined solely by the reference interpreter (§14.1) on
it; the object holds only the reference body; every executor
optimisation is extensionally equal on D (T6).

### 4.3 Allocation

| Instruction | Form | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| `qalloc` | `w = qalloc cls n` | n clean wires of class `cls` or `any`; unconditional |
| `qborrow` | `w = qborrow cls n` | n wires in unknown state; usable only inside a borrow frame (§5.6) |
| `qfree` | `qfree w` | release a `qalloc`ed bundle clean on every basis configuration |
| `qreturn` | `qreturn w` | matched inverse of `qborrow` after its frame |

In addition to §3.3's operand rules, `qfree d` is well-formed iff
every wire's current version is its `qalloc` version (including a
version restored by a matched inverse) or the clean output of an
explicit §6.4 cleanup of the value occupying that wire.
[deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.3-P1, ISA-5.1, ISA-6.4]

### 4.4 Gates

## 5. Regions

### 5.1 Interfaces and common well-formedness

Signatures list roles: `oracle @o (in a: wire[8]) (tgt t: wire[8]) (fault
f: wire[4]) (mem db: region[10,8] in) (args n: i64)`. **Interface
regions** are caller-owned, passed by name, role `in` (returned to entry
contents on every basis configuration) or `mut` (actions only). In the
text form (§16.2) a wire param's role suffix is optional and, when
present, MUST equal its group's role (`in` in an oracle's or action's
`in` group, `mut` in an action's `mut` group); a region param always
carries its role (`in` or `mut`); other region-interface params carry
no suffix. Lproc parameter roles are specified in §3.7 and §16.2. A region
MUST NOT contain `measure effect br call uncall trap hostcall qreg.* budget.mint` or
classical memory instructions, nor lifted rows outside oracle bodies.
Predicate stack empty at entry and exit. Every `qalloc` matched by a
`qfree` of a clean bundle, every `qborrow` by a `qreturn` after its
frame, every local `qregion` restored, by **explicit cleanup
instructions**; the schedule chooses only realisations. **`in` wires in
actions** appear only as controls (including the address operands of
`qload qunload qstore qexchm`, the wires pushed by `qpred`, the selector
of `qif`, whose two `x` gates restore it, and the `cond` wires of `qloop …
while`), as targets of diagonal gates, as `qcopy`/`qxor` sources, as the
value operand of `qstore` (it is only read, as the control of the XOR
into the selected cell), or as `in` arguments of calls. The **region
call graph** — an edge from a function to the callee of each of its
`oracle_call`, `action_call`, `lift.oracle_call`, `lift.oracle_uncall`
and `lift.perm_call` instructions and to both callees of each
`qinplace`, an oracle counting the rows of its lproc closure as its own —
is acyclic (suite F); recursion exists only through `lift.call` among
lprocs.

### 5.2 Lifted oracle

**Body**: lifted and control rows over its locations and lproc closure;
**exact-permutation only** (ε = 0 everywhere, transitively).

**Contract** on every x ∈ D: `ins` unchanged; `tgt` final = initial ⊕
payload(F_B(x)); `fr` final = initial ⊕ code(F_B(x)); `in` regions
unchanged; borrowed bundles returned (§5.6); workspace, locations, local
regions, step state clean / at image. **F_B(x)** = `(f(x), 0)` if the
reference interpreter reaches `lift.retval` within B steps without a
fault (f(x) = the XOR-accumulated `result_val`), else `(0, code)` with
`code` the first fault in sequential order, or `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` if
not halted after B steps. The oracle is an **involution** on `(ins, tgt,
fr)` and its own adjoint.

**Construction**: (1) allocate step state (`result_val` included),
locations, local regions, `alloctab` pre-entries, B frames; (2) run B
forward steps; (3) **commit**: `at_HALT = (pc == HALT)`; under `at_HALT ∧
¬faulted`: `qxor result_val tgt`; under `faulted`: `qxor code fr`; under
`¬at_HALT`: XOR the constant `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` into `fr`; uncompute
`at_HALT`; (4) **prefix reversal**: run the inverse step for t = B..1,
then release (1). The commit writes persist.

**Domain D**: every value of each `ins`, of `tgt`, of `fr`, of every cell
of every `in` region, of every borrowed bundle; `qalloc`ed wires at 0;
local regions at image.

### 5.3 Quantum action

**Semantics.** The body denotes a unitary U on mut ⊗ ins ⊗ mem ⊗
(borrowed), where mem includes both `mut` and `in` interface regions,
block-diagonal in the computational basis of `ins` and of `in`-region
cells, identity on borrowed and external reference systems (§5.6),
workspace returned clean, with domain D every value of each `mut`
and `in` bundle, every cell of every `mut` and `in` interface region and
every borrowed bundle, workspace initially at 0 and local regions at
image. [deps: ISA-5.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-5.6-P1, ISA-14.2] No fault outcome. Coherent internal
control yields the padded unitary under declared bounds, part of the
action's identity. **Permutation action**: realised unitary a
permutation matrix (ε = 0, permutation gates only, transitively).

**Body**: gates; `qcopy quncopy qxor qexch qinplace`; `qload qunload
qstore qexchm`; `qpred qunpred qif qloop`; `oracle_call action_call`;
`qalloc qborrow qfree qreturn`; `qregion`.
An action body with several `block`s executes its blocks in text order,
each a unitary fragment; labels are descriptive and carry no control
semantics, and lifetimes may span blocks.

**Bounds.** The `bounds { … }` list of an action's `regs` line and the
`bounds` array of its signature hold, in text order, the trip count `n`
of every `qloop` and `qloop … while` in the body (nested loops
included, outer before inner); the list MUST equal the body (suite F)
and is empty for a body without loops. It is part of the action's
identity because the padded unitary of coherent internal control
depends on it.

**Adjoint table** (reverse order, instruction replaced, versions
renumbered):

```
  rx/ry/rz(θ) ↦ (−θ)   s ↔ sdg   t ↔ tdg   x cx ccx mcx swap h cz: self-adjoint
  qalloc ↔ qfree   qborrow ↔ qreturn   qregion creation ↔ release   qcopy ↔ quncopy   qload ↔ qunload
  qxor qexch qstore qexchm: self-adjoint   qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins) (mem) (args) ↦ qinplace @ginv @g tk x (ins) (mem) (args)
  oracle_call ↦ oracle_call
  action_call @b ↦ adj action_call @b   qpred/qunpred pairs reversed as a whole
  qif w {A}{B} ↦ adjoint of the complete §4.6 expansion, including unconditional lifetimes, in reverse order. [deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.6-P7]
  qloop n {A} ↦ qloop n {adj A}
  qloop n while cond {A} ↦ flags computed forward as §4.6; adj A for i = n..1 under c_i; flags uncomputed
```

**Control.** `ctl w @a` is controlled-U: workspace, regions and flags
allocated unconditionally; every gate of the body and its cleanup runs
under `w` in addition to the active predicate.

The shorthand `qif w {adj A}{adj B}` denotes that adjoint when each
arm's allocations and releases are matched within that arm; no
arm-local lifetime restriction is imposed on the source `qif`.
[deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.6-P7, ISA-5.1]

Two operations form a **matched pair** iff the second is the §5.3
adjoint of the first, with the same callee(s) (the pair exchanged for
`qinplace`), identical classical argument values and token identities
and tuples, the same operand wires with the first's read-only input
versions and produced target versions current at the second, and equal
effective control sets including the versions of their wires; order and
repetition in predicate stacks are irrelevant. [deps: ISA-2-P10, ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-5.4]
A matched inverse restores preceding wire-version bindings for
subsequent matching only when the §6.2 cleanup conditions hold; matching
alone does not discharge an outstanding-reader or phase obligation, and
a cell write always advances its cell version even when its value is
restored. [deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-6.1-P1, ISA-6.2]

Scheduled action adjoints use the source replacements above before
reference-recipe instantiation, with the ordinal contract and `adj`
adapter specified in §11.1 (K62). [deps: ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P29]

### 5.4 Budgets and fault codes

Lproc token parameters forward existing tokens; they neither mint a
token nor alter its validity or tuple. Binding and forwarding have no
separate budget charge (§3.7); a lifted region call inside an lproc has
the same charge and token semantics as in an oracle body. In
particular, the enclosing oracle's token is not implicitly substituted
for a nested call's explicit token.

Budget = (B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit), classical. `bennett_
budget` validates: `heap_limit ≤ layout.heap_limit_max`, `stack_limit ≤
2^S_cells`, `depth_limit < 2^Dc − 1`; for a memoryless object (§7.2)
`heap_limit = stack_limit = depth_limit = 0`. B is enforced by running
exactly B steps; heap, stack and depth are wire counters.
*(non-normative, used by §4.5)* If the reference interpreter succeeds
on x under a tuple β₀, it succeeds with the same result under every
tuple β ≥ β₀ componentwise: its only limit-dependent decisions are the
flags of `lift.alloc` and `lift.call`, false on that trajectory and
still false with larger limits, and the step bound, not reached. A
nested `lift.oracle_call` row uses the tuple of its own token, not β,
which is why §4.5 fixes those tuples exactly.

**Tokens.** A token denotes a validated tuple (B, heap_limit,
stack_limit, depth_limit) and the oracle it was validated for; a region
call uses the tuple of the token it is given, and a token MAY be
supplied to any region call of the program (the checks above depend
only on the object's layout). Tokens come from `bennett_budget` (§10;
valid until the program is unloaded) or from **`tk = budget.mint @o s h
st d`** (CC and R): `@o` names an oracle of the program (a symbol
reference, §9.2) whose object's layout the checks use, and is recorded
with the token for diagnostics only; s, h, st, d are `i64` values read
as unsigned 64-bit;
the instruction applies exactly `bennett_budget`'s checks to (s, h, st,
d) before any mutation; failure is a fault record `FAULT_UB_TRAP` (§4.9)
and no token; success produces a fresh token for that tuple, valid
until its frame is reversed or its run is CLOSED, and never
constructible from an integer. A region call given a token that is not
valid is a CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP` before any effect (a launch
argument: `E_ARG`). A minted tuple binds its invocation exactly as a
host-minted one does (§11, §12): an invocation whose closure hash,
variant, inherited controls, tuple and `params_hash` equal the
`bindings` of the object's schedule executes that schedule; any other
invocation executes under the §11 defaults; a consumer MUST NOT apply a
schedule bound to another tuple, nor substitute another tuple. In
`params_hash` a token argument is encoded as its tuple
`[B,heap,stack,depth]`. A budget is classical and fixed before region entry (§3.1), so
`budget.mint` is not a region instruction (§5.1).

```
  0 FAULT_NONE            4 FAULT_UB_TRAP            8 FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED
  1 FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS    5 FAULT_UB_DIV0            9 FAULT_CALLEE
  2 FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP     6 FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW  10 FAULT_ENTROPY_EXHAUSTED
  3 FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH    7 FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT
```

F ≥ 4. Code 10 is recorded by the R `entropy` device (§7.3) and is also
the code a lifted oracle that virtualises entropy uses (`fault 10`) when
its entropy input is exhausted.

**Effective cause.** The literal C of `fault C` (in every tier, §4.2,
§4.9) and of `lift.faultif p C` records the cause C′ = C if C ≠ 0 and C′ =
`FAULT_UB_TRAP` (4) if C = 0;
every other row's code is a fixed nonzero constant. So no fault is ever
recorded as `FAULT_NONE`: a faulted outcome F_B(x) = (0, code) has code ≠
0, and the fault register distinguishes it from every success (F ≥ 4
makes 4 representable). Forward and inverse fault writes use C′.

### 5.5 Nesting and matched cleanup
